## ASSISTANT
I applied Sejal's fuller, squishier body to all 41 Cat v2 frames and replaced the art inside the existing Figma variants, so every node ID stays the same. The current cat is untouched. I checked the result in the comparison frame. Nobody has watched the loops run in Present yet.

**What changed:**
- **Standing and walking:** the rump is round and bulges behind the tail base, the back rises gently to the shoulders, and the belly sags 1 px between the legs. Legs are 5 px wide with soft rounded paws, so they no longer read as sticks.
- **Tail:** plumper, with a 5 px shaft and a 6 px round tip, in both the standing and sitting poses.
- **Sit:** a soft pear shape with round shoulders, wider haunches (26 px) and chunky rounded paws.
- **Sleep:** a rounder, squishier loaf with the plump tail curled at the front.
- **React:** the squash before the hop is now 2 px wider on each side as it gets shorter, so it feels squishy without the cat getting bigger.

The head stays 22 px and the cat stands about the same height, so it reads as a normal chunky cat, not a fat one. The face, eyes, whiskers and the 9 states are unchanged. With the thicker legs, the two legs of a pair still never touch during the walk.

The comparison frame and the v2 playground picked up the new art on their own, and I rewrote the row labels to match. I also updated `research.md`, the README, the hand-over note in the shared folder and my memory note. The new side-by-side image is in `preview/compare-v1-v2.png`.

- **Comparison:** https://www.figma.com/design/hfj2locm0N5bssdEU6NaoH/portfolio-2?node-id=35-564
- **v2 playground:** https://www.figma.com/design/hfj2locm0N5bssdEU6NaoH/portfolio-2?node-id=34-731

**How to review:**

- **Speed:** Half and Quarter slow it down. Pause, then use "Next frame" to step through one frame at a time.

- **Frame strip:** the small squares under each animation show every frame of that state at true size, with the one playing now outlined.

- **Yawn:** I'll replace the tall pink mouth with a wide, cat-like mouth showing small fangs.

- **React:** the hop uses the same mid-air pose.

Every change you asked for is in v2, and the review page at the same link is updated: https://claude.ai/artifact/V49VCbcdBkAtMHav3f29yx. The page-load demo plays first. The current cat in Figma is still untouched.

**What changed:**
- **Yawn:** the tall pink mouth is gone. The big yawn is now a small V-shaped cat mouth with two tiny fangs. I compared five mouth shapes side by side; wide pink shapes read as lips and tall ones as a tongue.
- **Sit:** the paws are tucked together into one solid, soft base, so the limbs no longer look held apart. Look, Yawn, React and Land use the same body, so they changed too.
- **Inspect:** the cat now faces the artwork side-on, notices it, and drops into a pounce crouch: chin low on its folded front paws, rump up, tail straight back. It wiggles its rump twice, then rises.
- **Walk:** each leg joins the body at its own spot, the same spots as in Idle. Legs no longer meet at a shared point, and no two legs touch in any frame.
- **Fall (new, for page load):** the cat falls upright in the landing pose, with all four legs out at about 30° and the tail straight and raised. It spins a full turn in 8 drawn frames: front, three-quarter, side profile, three-quarter back, back, then the same views mirrored. Nothing is rotated or tilted. Its eyes look down at where it will land.
- **Land (new):** its paws reach for the hero divider, it plops down with a squash, bounces a little, then sits.
- **React:** the hop now uses that same mid-air pose, with four legs out, the tail up and one heart.
- **Look and Sleep:** unchanged, as you approved them.

In Figma the Cat v2 set now has 58 frames. The v2 playground has new Fall and Land cells, and the comparison frame has new Fall and Land rows. The bigger set had started to overlap some frames the main portfolio session placed further right, so I moved my set into empty space below the v2 playground. Their frames weren't touched.

One thing I decided myself: after landing, the cat sits on the divider rather than standing. Tell me if you'd rather it stand.

## ASSISTANT
The five fixes are in, and the review page is updated at the same link: https://claude.ai/artifact/V49VCbcdBkAtMHav3f29yx. Figma is updated too, and the current cat is still untouched.

- **Land:** it now takes about 1.5 seconds instead of 0.3. The paws reach for the divider, the cat lands with a squash, holds still for half a second with its eyes open, blinks, stands up on four legs, then lowers into a sit.
- **Inspect:** I looked up how a cat crouches before it pounces. The sources agree on a low, coiled body, front paws reaching forward, back legs gathered underneath, and a twitching tail. None of them breaks it down frame by frame, so I combined that with your notes. The chin is now low, the front paws stretch out ahead of its face, and the back legs are tucked under the body. The tail is up and twitches. During the wiggle the back paws take turns lifting while the rump bobs, so the back legs no longer stay frozen.
- **Walk:** opposite legs now move together: front-left with back-right, then the other pair. Each leg still joins the body at its own spot, and the body bobs a pixel as it steps.
- **Peek:** the ears show first, then the cat pops up quickly, overshoots slightly, settles back down, and looks left and right slowly.
- **React:** the hop now has all four legs straight down under the body and the tail up, so the back legs no longer spread apart. The spread-legs pose stays only in the falling spin, which you approved.

The spin, the yawn and the peek pose are unchanged, as you approved them. In Figma, Peek and Land gained frames, and the labels in the v2 playground and the comparison frame are updated to match.

Sources:
- [Raw Paws: the cat hunting sequence](https://www.rawpawspetfood.com/blogs/raw-paws-blog/cat-hunting-sequence-stalk-chase-pounce-catch)
- [BGSU animation blog: cat pounce draft](https://blogs.bgsu.edu/artc413andjack/2008/09/22/draft/)

## ASSISTANT
The walk, the landing and the fall timing are fixed. The review page is updated at the same link: https://claude.ai/artifact/V49VCbcdBkAtMHav3f29yx. The current cat is still untouched.

**Why the walk looked wrong:** it already moved opposite legs together, but our cat is one flat colour, so the near and far legs looked identical. You couldn't tell which legs paired up, so it looked like all four legs went out and in at once. Your orange GIF avoids that by shading the far legs darker. Your pixel GIF shows the other thing you spotted: the rump flows straight into a thick back leg, and the front legs are thinner.

- **Walk:** the far-side legs are now a shade lighter on the black cat and a shade greyer on the white cat, so the front-near and back-far legs visibly step together, then the other pair.
- **Legs:** the back legs now have a thick thigh that grows out of the rump and tapers to the paw, with no notch at the top. The front legs are thinner.
- **Land:** it no longer sits twice. After the impact it stays bent down on all fours with its head low and eyes shut, opens its eyes and looks down, looks up, then rises into one normal sit.
- **Fall timing:** the page used to slow to a stop just before the cat's paws, which is why time seemed to freeze. Now it speeds up like a real fall, so the landing hits hard. The spin also finishes facing you a moment before touchdown and the paws reach down, so it no longer keeps turning until it hits the ground.

The lighter far-leg shade needed one new colour in the shared Figma colour collection, `cat/body-far`, with light and dark values like the other cat colours. Nothing else in the collection changed. The Figma walk and landing frames, the playground and the comparison frame are updated too.

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - Sprites:
     - 48x48 palette-indexed ASCII grids, built from stamps and spans in sprites.py.
     - TIMINGS in seconds per frame.
     - Feet sit on row 47 (GROUND).
   - Legend:
     - '.' empty, '#' body, 'f' far legs (lilac shade), 'l' top-light rim.
     - 's' ground shadow.
     - 'Y' eye ring, belt and marks; 'p' pupil (also the bell slot).
     - 'w' shine; 'm' and 'M' mouth.
     - 'z' Z; 'h' heart (unused now).
     - New key needed: 'g' (bell).
   - Colours come from Figma variables in the "Portfolio · Color" collection (VariableCollectionId:7:2; modes Dark 7:0, Light 7:1):
     - cat/* 7:10 to 7:17.
     - cat/body-far VariableID:75:817 (Dark #BEB7D2, Light #423B54).
   - Shape and motion:
     - The 8 px eye ring (RING) with a round fill; looking narrows the fill by 1 px (LOOK dict).
     - The standing pose is TRACED from her reference (cat-reference.webp) at about 7.4 px per cell. TRACE_BODY sits at TRACE_TOP=9, TRACE_LEFT=2; HX=19, HY=11.
     - Leg profiles: NEAR_HIND, FAR_HIND, NEAR_FRONT, FAR_FRONT.
     - limb() pivots at hip = 39 - up and shortens the leg for lift.
     - Diagonal-pair walk_feet.
   - Post-processing in FRAMES:
     - `NO_SHADOW = {'Fall','Tumble','Peek','PeekSide','PeekTop'}`.
     - `FRAMES = {k: [top_light(g if k in NO_SHADOW else ground_shadow(g)) for g in v] ...}`.
     - `FRAMES['Shadow'] = [shadow()]` (unused).
   - The Figma build encodes rects into a base64-like string with the alphabet `0-9a-zA-Z_-`, decoded into vectors bound to variables in use_figma.

3. Files and Code Sections:
   - ~/Documents/career/portfolio-pixel/cat-v2/sprites.py: the main source.

     - Collar code (inserted before head()):
       ```python
       BELL = [".YY.",
               "YYYY",
               ".pp."]
       def collar(g, mask):
           cols = {}
           for r, c in mask:
               cols[c] = max(cols.get(c, -1), r)
           span_cols = []
           for c, rb in sorted(cols.items()):
               if not (0 <= rb + 2 < H and 0 <= c < W):
                   continue
               below, below2 = g[rb + 1][c], g[rb + 2][c]
               if below in '#fl' or (below == '.' and below2 in '#fl'):
                   g[rb + 1][c] = 'Y'
                   span_cols.append((c, rb + 1))
           if len(span_cols) >= 4:
               c, r = span_cols[len(span_cols) // 2]
               stamp(g, BELL, r + 1, c - 1)
       def head(g, top, left, mode='open', look=(0, 0), whisk='both', ears='up', shine=True, belt=True):
           ... stamp HEAD, eyes, whiskers (WHISKERS = {11: 3, 13: 2}) ...
           if belt:
               collar(g, mask)
       ```
       - profile_head() calls collar with the PROFILE mask.
       - midair_34 calls collar after `stamp(g, HEAD, t, L)`.
     - Sphinx (current):
       ```python
       def sphinx(paw=0, mode='open', look=(1, 0)):
           g = blank()
           for r, c0, c1 in [(44, 25, 41), (45, 25, 42), (46, 25, 42)]: span(g, r, c0, c1, 'f')
           for r, c0, c1 in [(30, 6, 12), (31, 4, 15), (32, 3, 18)] + [(r, 2, 20) for r in range(33, 36)] + \
                             [(r, 2, 34) for r in range(36, 46)] + [(46, 3, 34), (47, 4, 33)]:
               span(g, r, c0, c1)
           for r, c0, c1 in [(44, 0, 3), (45, 0, 3), (46, 0, 4), (47, 0, 6)]: span(g, r, c0, c1)  # tail (merges, reads as missing)
           for r, c0, c1 in [(45 - paw, 28, 45), (46 - paw, 28, 46), (47 - paw, 29, 46)]: span(g, r, c0, c1)
           if paw:
               for r in range(45 - paw, 45): span(g, r, 28, 31)
           head(g, 19, 20, mode, look, whisk='right')
           return g
       ```
     - FRAMES:

<cross-session-message from="local_db62f9af-2f68-49fe-bf70-6ae5741c6604" name="Portfolio site code">
Code session: I'm building the cat wardrobe UI now. Here's the data format the code will read from cat-v2/preview/frames.json. If you've already chosen something else, tell me and I'll adapt.

Top level, next to "frames" and "timings":
"accessories": {
  "hat":     { "sprites": [ [rows...], ... ], "anchors": { "Sit": [[x, y, v], ...one entry per frame...], "Walk": [...], ... } },
  "tie":     { ...same shape... },
  "glasses": { ... },
  "bow":     { ... }
}
- sprites: one or more small row-string sprites, using the same palette keys as the cat ('.' means empty).
- anchors: for each cat state, one entry per frame. [x, y] is the top-left of the sprite in the unflipped 48x48 frame. The optional v picks which sprite to use (default 0). Use null for frames where the accessory should be hidden (a hat during Sleep, for example). A state you leave out means hidden in that whole state.
- The code mirrors accessories along with the cat when it faces left, so draw them facing right like the frames.

"icons": { "hat": [rows], "tie": [rows], "glasses": [rows], "bow": [rows] }
- 12x12 or 16x16, using '#' for filled pixels. The code tints them with the button's text colour.

Colours: please use the key 'k' for the glasses frames. Code maps it to --cat-glasses, which is white on the black cat (light theme) and black on the white cat (dark theme), so it swaps the moment the theme changes. For any other new colour key, tell me the key and the hex for light and dark.

The rule "hat hides bow" is handled in code. When the export is ready, message me and I'll import it, run the sweep and push to the revamp preview.
</cross-session-message>
